_source line 4654 · 2026-05-23T18:16:29.682Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a MAJOR finding for the Bayesian reversal. Let me analyze:

**Table 2 Corrected:**

| Method | η_sig (old→new) | K_sig (old→new) | K_dir (old→new) | R² (old→new) |
|--------|---|---|---|---|
| Threshold | 12→12 | 11→11 | 10+/1- → **11+/0-** | 0.89→0.829 |
| Majority | 9→**10** | 8→**12** | 7+/1- → **12+/0-** | 0.298→0.327 |


| Weighted | 9→9 | 9→**12** | 9+/0- → **12+/0-** | 0.284→0.328 |
| Bayesian | 9→9 | 7→6 | 1+/6- → **5+/1-** | 0.104→0.087 |

The Bayesian reversal is also a bug artifact—the old direction showed 1+/6- (majority negative), but after correction it shifts to 5+/1-, revealing the reversal was caused by the same underlying error affecting the other methods.

The earlier aggregation bootstrap that seemed to confirm the Bayesian reversal was also running on the buggy ASR version, so that validation was compromised. With the corrected data, Bayesian now shows mostly positive results like the other methods, which means the claim about Bayesian calibration causing a sign change is incorrect.

This undermines two of the paper's three main contributions — the attack-type taxonomy and the Bayesian reversal are both artifacts of the bug. What's left is evidence that a regime transition exists but happens gradually rather than sharply, plus the observation that K_eff significance increases with K.

The worker's interpretation claiming the reversal is "confirmed" and that judge diversity improves manipulation resistance is flatly wrong — the new data shows the opposite pattern entirely, with mostly positive coefficients instead of the original mostly negative ones.
```

</details>

K_eff 正向意味着更高 K_eff → 更高 ASR → 多样性**增加**脆弱性（不是提升抵抗力）\n\n## 受影响的论文位置（在之前清单基础上新增）\n\n之前列出的 12 处 6:6 split 文本 + 以下新增：\n- **results.tex:91-102** — Table 2 全部数字\n- **results.tex:102** — \"Bayesian calibration is associated with a sign change in the diversity channel: K_eff direction shifts from predominantly positive (7+/1-) to predominantly negative (1+/6-)\" → **完全不成立**\n- **analysis.tex:21** — \"Bayesian calibration is associated with a reversal of K_eff's direction at K=3 (6/7 negative)\" → **完全不成立**\n- **conclusion.tex:3** — \"Bayesian calibration corresponds to a sign change at K=3\" → **完全不成立**\n- **introduction.tex:16** — \"Bayesian calibration corresponding to a sign change\"\n- **introduction.tex:22** — Contribution 3 中的 Bayesian sign change\n\n## 建议\n\n修正后论文仍可 salvage 的 story：\n1. **Regime transition（弱化版）**：从 \"both contribute\" 到 \"only K_eff\" 的渐进转变\n2. **K_eff 一致正向**：多样性在所有 K、所有攻击类型、所有聚合方法下都增加脆弱性——这实际上是一个 **更简洁、更 clean** 的 finding\n3. **Bayesian 降低可预测性**：虽然不反转方向，但 R² 从 0.33 降到 0.087，说明 Bayesian 确实改变了 panel 组成与脆弱性的关系强度\n\n这个修正后的 story 对 EMNLP Findings 仍有价值，但需要坦诚 reframe。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a major assessment request. Let me think through each question carefully based on all the data I've collected.

**Current state after V2 correction:**

Contributions status:
1. Regime transition: K_eff 4→10→9, η_max 3→0→0 — EXISTS but gradual, not sharp
2. 6:6 sign split: DEAD (12/12 positive)
3. Bayesian reversal: DEAD (5+/1-, not 1+/6-)

What survives:


- K_eff shows consistent monotonic increase in significance as K grows, while η_max monotonically decreases
- Beyond K=5, K_eff dominates the predictions entirely
- K_eff maintains positive coefficients across every condition, every K value, and every aggregation method tested
- The aggregation methods show a clear vulnerability gradient where threshold-based approaches (R²=0.83) are most robust, followed by majority and weighted voting (both R²≈0.33), with Bayesian methods being most fragile (R²=0.09)
- Bayesian aggregation degrades predictability but never flips the direction of effects
- At K=3, K_eff reaches significance in 4 out of 12 conditions, all involving shared-bias scenarios
- Now examining the confound analysis across Pool A and Cinelli conditions

—these remain unaffected by the bug
- The random versus adaptive fault contrast needs verification to see if it follows the same pattern
- Cross-benchmark instrumental variable analysis was derived from pre-computed results, so I need to validate that extraction

For the EMNLP Findings threshold, the regime transition finding is solid: at K=3 both η_max and K_eff predict vulnerability, but at K≥5 only K_eff survives. The corrected data actually strengthens this—K_eff shows significance in all 12 OLS conditions and 10 out of 12 at K=5, which is a genuine empirical contribution meeting the bar for novel findings with sound methodology.

The real question is whether this alone carries a paper, even for Findings. The core insight—that K_eff dominates as panel size increases while η_max fades—is somewhat predictable from theory. But paired with the confound analysis showing the 0.70-0.81 correlation and Pool A mitigation, the aggregation vulnerability gradient dropping from 0.83 to 0.09 R², the K_eff framework itself, and the wild cluster bootstrap methodology for combinatorial designs, there's enough substance here. It's borderline, but defensible.

Now looking at what's actually new in the V2 data: K=3 shows K_eff significance in only 4 out of 12 conditions, which is notably different from the stronger pattern at higher K values.

What's striking is that K_eff's effect appears attack-type-dependent in strength—it consistently matters across shared-bias attacks like sycophancy, verbosity bias, and authority bias on MMLU, plus verbosity bias on ARC. The corrected Table 1 reveals K_eff's standardized coefficients are actually larger than η_max's across many conditions, which flips the original narrative where η_max seemed dominant. The aggregation gradient persists even under Bayesian calibration, where R² drops to 0.087 and K_eff significance falls to 6 out of 12—the dramatic loss of predictability itself is the finding, showing that Bayesian methods decouple panel composition from vulnerability. Position bias remains the outlier with the smallest K_eff slopes.

Now I'm working through what the corrected story actually is: diversity consistently drives panel vulnerability across all conditions, but the relative importance of individual weakness versus structural diversity shifts with panel size—at K=3 both matter, but at K≥5 diversity structure dominates while individual weakness drops out entirely. The aggregation method acts as a moderator, with threshold voting creating near-deterministic outcomes while Bayesian calibration substantially reduces that predictability.

The core tension is that diversity and capability are structurally correlated (r=0.70–0.81), which makes it hard to isolate causal effects even with capability-matched subsampling. But the findings themselves are robust, and the practical takeaway—avoid maximizing diversity and instead screen for robustness—still stands. I'm now weighing whether these corrected results are compelling enough to move forward with EMNLP 2026, given that the methodology is solid and the framework offers something novel.

The main risk is that losing the sign-flip and Bayesian reversal findings weakens the narrative considerably. The story becomes "diversity consistently hurts, especially at larger K," which is less surprising than the original claim. But actually, stepping back, the fact that diversity *always* hurts runs counter to conventional wisdom around collective intelligence and diversity benefits—that's itself a meaningful negative result worth publishing, and honest framing of that could be the angle that makes this work for EMNLP Findings.

The regime transition, though less dramatic than before, still offers novel insight into *why* this happens mechanistically. Combined with the diversity paradox framing and the confound analysis presented as methodological rigor rather than weakness, this could be a solid Findings paper. Whether it reaches Main depends entirely on how well I can reframe the contribution around this counterintuitive finding.
```

```text
This is a minor finding — no need for a critical or warning review. Just confirming info.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01F4WDzjs3Nkx6wm7e5iQSG1`</summary>

```text
1	# V2 Corrected Data Inventory
2	Generated: 2026-05-23
3	
4	Total files: 18 JSON files
5	V1→V2 corrected: 5 files (table1, table2, regime_transition, keff_distribution, random_fault_contrast)
6	New analyses: 13 files
7	
8	---
9	
10	## Table 1 — OLS Dominance (Section 4.1)
11	
12	- **table1_corrected.json**: V1→V2 corrected. OLS log-log, K=3, 455 panels. Error-as-tie bug fix (1221/63000 = 1.94% errors treated as ties). K_eff sig: 8/12→**12/12**; eta sig: 11/12→10/12. Dominance distribution shifts from {Co:7, eta:4, K:1} to {Co:10, K:2, eta:0}. 5 conditions changed dominance category. Key finding: K_eff universally significant and always positive after correction.
13	
14	- **epsilon_sensitivity.json**: Epsilon sensitivity for K=3. OLS coefficients at ε=1e-3, 1e-6, 1e-9. Shows K_eff coefficients are sensitive to epsilon choice in conditions with high ASR-zero rates (e.g., arc_sycophancy: K_eff beta flips sign across epsilon). Includes per-condition ASR zero rates for K3/K5/K7.
15	
16	- **epsilon_sensitivity_k5.json**: Epsilon sensitivity for K=5. Max coefficient change: K_eff **184.5%**, eta 31.07%. At ε=1e-6: K_eff sig 12/12, eta sig 12/12 (all conditions significant at K=5). Large N (3003 panels) stabilizes estimates.
17	
18	## Table 2 — Aggregation Methods (Section 4.2)
19	
20	- **table2_corrected.json**: V1→V2 corrected. K=3, 455 panels, 4 aggregation methods. All 4 methods changed after error filtering. Majority: K_eff sig 8/12→**12/12**, dir 7+/1-→**12+/0-**. Weighted: K_eff sig 9/12→**12/12**. Bayesian: direction reversed 1+/6-→**5+/1-** (old negative was artifact). Threshold: spurious negative disappeared 10+/1-→11+/0-.
21	
22	- **aggregation_bootstrap.json**: Bootstrap validation of Table 2. K=3, B=9999. Covers majority/weighted/threshold/bayesian. Confirms OLS directions: majority 11/12 positive, weighted 11/12 positive, threshold 11/12 positive. Bayesian reversal confirmed: 9/12 negative, 0% positive rate on 6 strongest conditions. NOTE: these are V1 (pre-correction) OLS baselines with bootstrap CIs.
23	
24	## Regime Transition (Section 4.3)
25	
26	- **regime_transition_corrected.json**: V1→V2 corrected. Wild cluster bootstrap (B=9999, Rademacher, max-p, BH FDR 0.05). K3: K_eff sig 1→**4**, eta sig 5→3. K5: K_eff sig 7→**10**. K7: K_eff sig 5→**9**. Per-condition FDR-adjusted p-values included. 1365 filtered samples. All K sizes changed.
27	
28	## GAM Nonlinear Analysis (Section 4.4 / Appendix)
29

39
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a comprehensive data inventory. Let me review it for accuracy and consistency with what I know.

Key observations:

1. **gam_magnitude_gradient.json** — New analysis I haven't seen before. Shows Kruskal-Wallis test of GAM slope between targeted vs shared-bias: K3 p=0.200, K5 p=0.423. **No significant difference** in slope magnitude between attack categories. This means even the "magnitude gradient" I suggested as a replacement for the 6:6 split doesn't hold statistically!

2. **aggregation_bootstrap.json** — The inventory correctly notes "these are V1 (pre-correction) OLS baselines with bootstrap CIs." This confirms the bootstrap "confirmation" of Bayesian reversal was V1 buggy data.
   
3. **keff_distribution_corrected.json** — The K_eff values jumped substantially after correction (K3: 1.92→2.18, K5: 2.32→3.35), which is strange since both versions should load from the same MI matrix. Looking closer, I see the uncorrected version uses MI-based estimation while the corrected one uses empirical Gaussian copula MI averaged across MMLU & ARC—so they're actually using different MI estimation methods, which explains the discrepancy.

4. **epsilon_sensitivity_k5.json** — The K_eff coefficients shift by up to 184.5% across epsilon values at K=5, which is troubling given how sensitive the results are to this parameter. Though with N=3003, everything registers as statistically significant.

5. **sensitivity_analysis_poolA.json** — RV exceeds eta in 9 out of 24 conditions now, compared to 8 out of 36 earlier, so Pool A results might also need the V2 verification.

6. **V2 Correction Summary** claims the regime transition becomes more pronounced, but that's misleading—the transition is actually smoother (K_eff ranges shift from 1→7 to 4→10), not sharper. What's strengthened is K_eff's overall effect, not the sharpness of the transition itself.

I need to flag that **aggregation_bootstrap.json** is still V1 data, so if the paper relies on those results, they'll need to be recomputed with V2. Also, **gam_magnitude_gradient.json** shows no significant difference in GAM slope between targeted and shared-bias conditions (p>0.2), which means the magnitude gradient alternative I proposed for Contribution 2 isn't actually supported by the data. The V2 results show K_eff is consistently positive across conditions with no meaningful differentiation by attack type—neither in direction nor in magnitude.
```
